N1 神经氨酸酶单克隆抗体 2H08 和 3H03 对 2018 年后流行的 H1N1 流感病毒的结合和抑制特性降低

N1 neuraminidase monoclonal antibodies 2H08 and 3H03 have decreased binding and inhibition properties against H1N1 influenza viruses circulating after 2018
作者： Bhavsar, D., Hermann, E., Monahan, B., Nardulli, J., Gleason, C., Simon, V., Krammer, F.
摘要中文翻译流感病毒是全球公共卫生问题。疫苗 可用,但由于 疫苗 主要成分表面糖蛋白血凝素 (HA) 的抗原漂移,必须每年更新。该病毒的第二种表面糖蛋白神经氨酸酶(NA)最近成为 疫苗 开发的焦点。然而,其抗原 进化 尚未得到充分研究。我们发现,两种具有广泛保护性的 N1 NA 抗体(之前已被证明可以结合并抑制大多数 N1 NA)对季节性 H1N1 病毒的近期 N1 失去了活性。在这里,我们表明,活性的下降主要归因于静电表面电位从中性到带正电的变化。在测试历史人类血浆样本时没有观察到这种效应,这表明活性下降是两种测试的单克隆抗体所特有的,但不会广泛影响多克隆人类反应。最后,我们确认测试的单克隆抗体对最近出现的泛动物进化枝 2.3.4.4b H5N1 NA 具有完全活性。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
流感病毒是全球公共卫生问题。该研究旨在调查 N1 神经氨酸酶单克隆抗体针对 2018 年之后流行的 H1N1 流感病毒的有效性变化。
设计与对象
该研究是使用针对 H1N1 流感病毒的单克隆抗体 2H08 和 3H03 进行的。它涉及针对最近的病毒分离株测试这些抗体,并将其活性与历史样本进行比较。
核心方法
通过测试历史人类血浆样本来评估活性下降的影响,该样本没有显示出这种变化,这表明活性下降是两种测试的单克隆抗体所特有的,而不是多克隆人类反应的总体趋势。
主要结果
我们发现,两种具有广泛保护性的 N1 NA 抗体(之前已被证明可以结合并抑制大多数 N1 NA)对季节性 H1N1 病毒的近期 N1 失去了活性。
解释与创新
活性下降主要归因于静电表面电位从中性到带正电的变化,这是一个新发现,强调了 N1 神经氨酸酶的抗原性 进化。
科研与公卫意义
这一发现对 疫苗 和针对 N1 神经氨酸酶的治疗抗体的开发具有影响,因为它表明需要更新策略来解释循环 H1N1 病毒的抗原变化。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于抽象证据,并且研究结果特定于两种测试的单克隆抗体,表明对结论的可信度中等。

N1 neuraminidase monoclonal antibodies 2H08 and 3H03 have decreased binding and inhibition properties against H1N1 influenza viruses circulating after 2018

Authors: Bhavsar, D., Hermann, E., Monahan, B., Nardulli, J., Gleason, C., Simon, V., Krammer, F.
Original abstract (English)
Influenza viruses are global public health problem. Vaccines are available but have to be updated annually due to antigenic drift of the main vaccine component, the surface glycoprotein hemagglutinin (HA). The second surface glycoprotein of the virus, neuraminidase (NA), has recently moved into the spotlight of vaccine development. However, its antigenic evolution is understudied. We found that two broadly protective N1 NA antibodies, which had previously been shown to bind and inhibit most N1 NA, lost activity to recent N1s from seasonal H1N1 viruses. Here, we show that this drop in activity can mostly be attributed to a change in electrostatic surface potential from neutral to positively charged. This effect was not observed when historic human plasma samples were tested, suggesting that the drop of activity is specific to the two tested mAbs but does not broadly impact the polyclonal human response. Finally, we confirmed that the tested mAbs were fully active against recently emerged pan-zootic clade 2.3.4.4b H5N1 NA.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Influenza viruses are global public health problem. The study aimed to investigate the changing effectiveness of N1 neuraminidase monoclonal antibodies against H1N1 influenza viruses circulating after 2018.
Design and population
The study was conducted using monoclonal antibodies 2H08 and 3H03 against H1N1 influenza viruses. It involved testing these antibodies against recent viral isolates and comparing their activity to historic samples.
Core methods
The effect of the drop in activity was assessed by testing historic human plasma samples, which showed no such change, indicating that the loss of activity is specific to the two tested mAbs and not a general trend in the polyclonal human response.
Main results
We found that two broadly protective N1 NA antibodies, which had previously been shown to bind and inhibit most N1 NA, lost activity to recent N1s from seasonal H1N1 viruses.
Interpretation and novelty
The drop in activity can mostly be attributed to a change in electrostatic surface potential from neutral to positively charged, which is a novel finding highlighting the antigenic evolution of N1 neuraminidase.
Scientific and public-health significance
This finding has implications for the development of vaccines and therapeutic antibodies targeting N1 neuraminidase, as it suggests the need for updated strategies to account for antigenic changes in circulating H1N1 viruses.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to abstract-only evidence, and the findings are specific to the two tested mAbs, suggesting moderate confidence in the conclusions.

划分和减少:农场内分区对家禽 禽流感 传播 影响的系统回顾和数学建模

Divide and reduce: systematic review and mathematical modelling of the impact of within-farm compartmentalisation on avian influenza transmission in poultry
作者： Sophie Planchand, Christelle Nivoix, Egil A.J. Fischer, Timothee Vergne, Sébastien Lambert
摘要中文翻译

范围与问题
提供的证据没有明确说明审查范围或中心问题。
证据基础与方法
150-200 words): Airborne transmission of avian influenza viruses (AIVs) is animportant transmission route among domestic poultry, but less effective than the classical faecal-oral route.Therefore, flock compartmentalisation, consisting in physically separating birds into groups, is expected to reduce the spread of AIVs in poultry.In this study, we quantified the impact of compartmentalisation on within-flock AIV spread by (i) performing a systematic review of transmission experiments studying airborne transmission of AIVs and(ii) reanalysing the data from 30 experiments in 13 eligible studies with mathematical models.When compared to transmission between birds in the same pen, between-pen transmission was systematically reduced for highly pathogenic AIVs (median reduction: 96%, min: 83%max: 100%).For low pathogenicity AIVs, 22/26 estimates indicated a reduced between-pen transmission, but results ranged from 100% reduction to 160% increase.Estimated parameters were then used in a deterministic mechanistic model to illustrate the potential impact of compartmentalisation on highly pathogenic AIVs transmission in field conditions, showing https://doi.org/10.

SARS、H1N1、埃博拉、COVID-19、MPOX、H5N1 和汉坦病毒:人类正在进入流行病的新时代吗?土耳其准备好迎接下一个全球 暴发 了吗?

SARS, H1N1, EBOLA, COVID-19, MPOX, H5N1 AND HANTAVIRUS: IS HUMANITY ENTERING A NEW ERA OF PANDEMIC? IS TÜRKİYE PREPARED FOR THE NEXT GLOBAL OUTBREAK?
作者： Sebla Ak
摘要中文翻译传染病的出现和再次出现对全球卫生安全构成持续挑战。暴发,例如严重急性呼吸系统综合症 (SARS)、甲型流感 (H1N1)、埃博拉病毒病、2019 年冠状病毒病 (COVID-19)、mpox、禽流感 A(H5N1) 和汉坦病毒感染,突出表明需要有效防范新出现的传染病威胁。“X疾病”的概念进一步强调了为可能导致未来流行病或大流行的未知病原体做好准备的重要性。本研究旨在检查主要传染病 暴发 并评估土耳其对未来潜在的全球 暴发 的准备情况。基于对科学出版物和机构报告的结构化文献检索和文档分析,采用了描述性综述设计。使用 SWOT 框架对调查结果进行组织和解释。该分析将医疗基础设施、重症监护能力、数字医疗系统以及在 COVID-19 大流行期间获得的经验确定为潜在优势。然而,与医疗保健劳动力能力、人畜共患疾病、公共卫生素养、人类、动物和环境卫生部门之间的协调以及某些生物技术产品对外部来源的依赖相关的挑战可能会限制准备工作。气候变化、环境破坏、国际旅行、移民和全球贸易被认为是可能影响传染病出现和传播的因素。加强综合 监测 和早期预警系统,通过“同一个健康”方法改善部门间协调,提高国内 疫苗 和诊断生产能力,以及扩大基于社区的公共卫生教育,可能有助于提高土耳其对未来 暴发 的准备。这些发现凸显了针对新出现的传染病威胁进行持续、多部门准备的重要性。

SARS, H1N1, EBOLA, COVID-19, MPOX, H5N1 AND HANTAVIRUS: IS HUMANITY ENTERING A NEW ERA OF PANDEMIC? IS TÜRKİYE PREPARED FOR THE NEXT GLOBAL OUTBREAK?

Authors: Sebla Ak
Original abstract (English)
The emergence and re-emergence of infectious diseases pose persistent challenges to global health security. Outbreaks such as severe acute respiratory syndrome (SARS), influenza A(H1N1), Ebola virus disease, coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19), mpox, avian influenza A(H5N1), and hantavirus infections highlight the need for effective preparedness against emerging infectious threats. The concept of “Disease X” further emphasizes the importance of preparing for unknown pathogens that may cause future epidemics or pandemics. This study aimed to examine major infectious disease outbreaks and assess Türkiye’s preparedness for potential future global outbreaks. A descriptive review design was employed, based on a structured literature search and document analysis of scientific publications and institutional reports. The findings were organized and interpreted using a SWOT framework. The analysis identified healthcare infrastructure, intensive care capacity, digital health systems, and experience gained during the COVID-19 pandemic as potential strengths. However, challenges related to healthcare workforce capacity, zoonotic disease surveillance, public health literacy, coordination across human, animal, and environmental health sectors, and dependence on external sources for certain biotechnological products may constrain preparedness. Climate change, environmental disruption, international travel, migration, and global trade were identified as factors that may influence the emergence and spread of infectious diseases. Strengthening integrated surveillance and early warning systems, improving intersectoral coordination through the One Health approach, enhancing domestic vaccine and diagnostic production capacity, and expanding community-based public health education may help improve Türkiye’s preparedness for future outbreaks. These findings highlight the importance of sustained, multisectoral preparedness for emerging infectious disease threats.

These findings highlight the importance of sustained, multisectoral preparedness for emerging infectious disease threats.

宿主 禽流感 病毒复制和跨物种适应中的 ANP32A 因子:叙述综述

Host Factor ANP32A in Avian Influenza Virus Replication and Cross-Species Adaptation: A Narrative Review
作者： Zeyu Li, Shuang Zhao, Zhi Cao, Jiuzhou Song, Hongyan Sun, Qisheng Zuo, Wei Han, Guohong Chen, Bichun Li, Kai Jin
摘要中文翻译高致病性禽流感病毒经历持续的进化,并增强了跨物种的传播能力,对全球公共卫生安全构成严重威胁。宿主 酸性核磷蛋白 32A (ANP32A) 作为激活流感病毒 RNA 依赖性 RNA 聚合酶的关键 宿主 辅因子,在病毒复制和 宿主 适应中发挥核心作用。该综述系统阐述了ANP32A的分子特征,及其富含亮氨酸重复序列(LRR)结构域特异性结合聚合酶碱性蛋白2(PB2)亚基及其低复杂性酸性区(LCAR)与聚合酶酸性蛋白(PA)相互作用以促进病毒聚合酶复合物组装和功能激活的机制。它还总结了ANP32A的物种特异性序列变异和剪接亚型对跨物种传播的宿主限制屏障的影响。进一步分析表明,ANP32A通过染色质表观遗传修饰和转录资源竞争,间接调节干扰素、NF-κB和NOD样受体pyrin结构域3(NLRP3)炎症小体相关基因的转录水平,重塑细胞抗病毒药物微环境作为核转录调节因子。ANP32A和酸性核磷蛋白32B(ANP32B)发挥互补功能支持病毒聚合酶活性,而酸性核磷蛋白32E(ANP32E)仅对一小部分突变流感毒株提供微弱的辅助作用,其主要生理功能以染色质调节为中心;这三种蛋白质共同参与病毒复制的调节网络。借鉴本综述中叙述性讨论的冷冻电镜结构生物学和 CRISPR/Cas9 基因编辑技术的最新进展,本综述强调了 ANP32A 在病毒 宿主 适应和跨物种 跨物种溢出 中的关键枢纽作用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注 宿主 因子 ANP32A 在 禽流感 病毒复制和跨物种适应中的作用。核心问题是ANP32A如何促进病毒复制并影响宿主 A病毒的宿主适应。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对现有文献的叙述性综合,讨论了 ANP32A 的分子特征、其与病毒聚合酶成分的相互作用,以及序列变异和剪接亚型对跨物种 传播 的影响。它不报告纳入研究的荟萃分析或具体资格标准。
共识与结论
ANP32A 是一种关键的 宿主 辅因子,可激活流感病毒 RNA 依赖性 RNA 聚合酶,在病毒复制和 宿主 适应中发挥核心作用。其 LRR 结构域与 PB2 结合,其 LCAR 与 PA 相互作用,以促进病毒聚合酶复合物的组装和功能激活。此外,ANP32A 间接调节干扰素、NF-κB 和 NLRP3 炎性体相关基因的转录水平,重塑细胞 抗病毒药物 微环境。
争议与缺口
该综述强调需要进一步研究 ANP32A 的物种特异性序列变异和剪接亚型影响跨物种 传播 的 宿主 限制屏障的具体机制。在 禽流感 背景下,还缺乏对 ANP32A 与其他 宿主 因子之间功能相互作用的详细研究。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应探索 ANP32A 在病毒复制和 宿主 适应中的分子机制,特别是与物种特异性变异和剪接亚型相关的分子机制。此外,开发破坏 ANP32A-病毒聚合酶相互作用的靶向 抗病毒药物 策略可能是治疗干预的一个有前景的领域。

Host Factor ANP32A in Avian Influenza Virus Replication and Cross-Species Adaptation: A Narrative Review

Authors: Zeyu Li, Shuang Zhao, Zhi Cao, Jiuzhou Song, Hongyan Sun, Qisheng Zuo, Wei Han, Guohong Chen, Bichun Li, Kai Jin
Original abstract (English)
Highly pathogenic avian influenza virus undergoes continuous evolution with strengthened cross-species transmission capacity, posing a severe threat to global public health security. Host acidic nuclear phosphoprotein 32A (ANP32A) acts as a critical host cofactor for activating influenza viral RNA-dependent RNA polymerase, and plays a central role in viral replication and host adaptation. This review systematically elaborates the molecular features of ANP32A, as well as the mechanism by which its leucine-rich repeat (LRR) domain specifically binds to the polymerase basic protein 2 (PB2) subunit and its low-complexity acidic region (LCAR) interacts with polymerase acidic protein (PA) to facilitate the assembly and functional activation of viral polymerase complexes. It also summarizes the influences of species-specific sequence variations and splicing isoforms of ANP32A on the host restriction barrier of cross-species transmission. Further analyses reveal that ANP32A indirectly modulates the transcriptional levels of genes related to interferons, NF-κB and NOD-like receptor pyrin domain-containing 3 (NLRP3) inflammasomes via chromatin epigenetic modification and transcriptional resource competition, remodeling the cellular antiviral microenvironment as a nuclear transcriptional regulator. ANP32A and acidic nuclear phosphoprotein 32B (ANP32B) exert complementary functions to support viral polymerase activity, while acidic nuclear phosphoprotein 32E (ANP32E) only provides weak auxiliary effects on a small subset of mutant influenza strains with its primary physiological function centered on chromatin regulation; the three proteins jointly participate in the regulatory network of viral replication. Drawing on recent advances in cryo-electron microscopy structural biology and CRISPR/Cas9 gene-editing technologies, which are discussed narratively in this review, this review highlights the pivotal hub role of ANP32A in viral host adaptation and cross-species spillover.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the role of host factor ANP32A in avian influenza virus replication and cross-species adaptation. The central question is how ANP32A facilitates viral replication and influences the host adaptation of avian influenza A viruses.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis of existing literature, discussing molecular features of ANP32A, its interactions with viral polymerase components, and the impact of sequence variations and splicing isoforms on cross-species transmission. It does not report on meta-analysis or specific eligibility criteria for included studies.
Consensus and conclusions
ANP32A is a critical host cofactor that activates the influenza viral RNA-dependent RNA polymerase, playing a central role in viral replication and host adaptation. Its LRR domain binds to PB2, and its LCAR interacts with PA to facilitate the assembly and functional activation of viral polymerase complexes. Additionally, ANP32A indirectly modulates the transcriptional levels of genes related to interferons, NF-κB, and NLRP3 inflammasomes, remodeling the cellular antiviral microenvironment.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights the need for further research on the specific mechanisms by which species-specific sequence variations and splicing isoforms of ANP32A influence the host restriction barrier for cross-species transmission. There is also a lack of detailed studies on the functional interactions between ANP32A and other host factors in the context of avian influenza.
Research and practice implications
Future research should explore the molecular mechanisms of ANP32A in viral replication and host adaptation, particularly in relation to species-specific variations and splicing isoforms. Additionally, the development of targeted antiviral strategies that disrupt ANP32A-viral polymerase interactions could be a promising area for therapeutic intervention.

基于 PCR 的 禽流感 病毒检测方法的诊断准确性:系统评价和荟萃分析

Diagnostic Accuracy of PCR-Based Methods for Avian Influenza Virus Detection: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis
作者： Cielithea Abegail C. Landayan
摘要中文翻译本研究旨在系统回顾和荟萃分析基于聚合酶链式反应 (PCR) 的 禽流感 病毒 (AIV) 检测方法的诊断准确性。随着 禽流感 暴发 对家禽业和公共卫生的持续威胁,准确、快速的诊断工具对于早期检测、监测 和 暴发 控制至关重要。该系统评价将评估各种基于 PCR 的检测(包括 RT-PCR、实时 RT-PCR 和多重 PCR)与病毒分离和血清学检测等参考标准相比的敏感性、特异性和整体诊断性能。研究将通过电子数据库(例如 PubMed、Scopus、Web of Science 和 Embase)的综合检索来确定。这项荟萃分析的结果将为最可靠的基于 PCR 的 AIV 检测诊断方法提供基于证据的见解,为临床和兽医诊断实践以及公共卫生 监测 策略提供信息。

范围与问题
本研究旨在系统回顾和荟萃分析基于聚合酶链式反应 (PCR) 的 禽流感 病毒 (AIV) 检测方法的诊断准确性。
证据基础与方法
该系统评价将评估各种基于 PCR 的检测(包括 RT-PCR、实时 RT-PCR 和多重 PCR)与病毒分离和血清学检测等参考标准相比的敏感性、特异性和整体诊断性能。

研究将通过电子数据库(例如 PubMed、Scopus、Web of Science 和 Embase)的综合检索来确定。
争议与缺口

随着 禽流感 暴发 对家禽业和公共卫生的持续威胁,准确、快速的诊断工具对于早期检测、监测 和 暴发 控制至关重要。

Diagnostic Accuracy of PCR-Based Methods for Avian Influenza Virus Detection: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis

Authors: Cielithea Abegail C. Landayan
Original abstract (English)
This study aims to systematically review and meta-analyze the diagnostic accuracy of polymerase chain reaction (PCR)-based methods for the detection of avian influenza virus (AIV). With the ongoing threat of avian influenza outbreaks to both poultry industries and public health, accurate and rapid diagnostic tools are critical for early detection, surveillance, and outbreak control. This systematic review will evaluate the sensitivity, specificity, and overall diagnostic performance of various PCR-based assays (including RT-PCR, real-time RT-PCR, and multiplex PCR) compared to reference standards such as viral isolation and serological testing. Studies will be identified through comprehensive searches of electronic databases (e.g., PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, and Embase). The findings of this meta-analysis will provide evidence-based insights into the most reliable PCR-based diagnostic methods for AIV detection, informing clinical and veterinary diagnostic practices, as well as public health surveillance strategies.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study aims to systematically review and meta-analyze the diagnostic accuracy of polymerase chain reaction (PCR)-based methods for the detection of avian influenza virus (AIV).
Evidence base and method
This systematic review will evaluate the sensitivity, specificity, and overall diagnostic performance of various PCR-based assays (including RT-PCR, real-time RT-PCR, and multiplex PCR) compared to reference standards such as viral isolation and serological testing.
Consensus and conclusions
Studies will be identified through comprehensive searches of electronic databases (e.g., PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, and Embase).
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not clearly state controversies or evidence gaps.
Research and practice implications
With the ongoing threat of avian influenza outbreaks to both poultry industries and public health, accurate and rapid diagnostic tools are critical for early detection, surveillance, and outbreak control.

益生菌作为缓解禽类病毒感染的策略:免疫调节机制与治疗潜力

Probiotics as a Strategy for Mitigating Viral Infections in Poultry: Immunomodulatory Mechanisms and Therapeutic Potential.
作者： Elmer Nayra Ametefe, Daniel Saka, Juliet Nuerkie Anang, Lourraine Sena Doh, Leonard Kofi Adjorlolo
摘要中文翻译禽类病毒感染仍然是禽类养殖业中的持续挑战,导致显著的经济损失并可能对人类健康构成潜在威胁。涉及病原体的病毒感染频率不断上升,例如传染性支气管炎病毒(IBV)、新城疫病毒(NDV)和禽流感病毒(AIV),凸显了对有效干预措施的迫切需求。由于益生菌在增强免疫应答和建立健康肠道微生物群方面具有潜力,它们已成为一种有前景的策略,用于对抗这些病毒感染。通过在Google Scholar、Scopus和PubMed上使用关键搜索词,如'益生菌'、'乳酸杆菌'、'嗜酸乳杆菌'、'禽类'、'病毒感染'、'新城疫病毒(NDV)'及其他相关术语进行了文献检索。所选文献的纳入标准基于其与禽类益生菌干预措施的相关性、对免疫机制的关注以及对西非禽类养殖系统中常见病毒病原体的分析。本综述探讨了益生菌在减轻NDV、AIV和IBV对禽类影响中的作用,重点在于其免疫调节效应和抗病毒机制。嗜酸乳杆菌(Lactobacillus acidophilus)和植物乳杆菌(L. plantarum)在调节免疫参数和增强抗体反应方面显示出对抗NDV感染的有趣前景。同样,这些益生菌在对抗AIV方面也表现出抗病毒活性,促进免疫激活并降低病毒载量。植物乳杆菌在应对IBV病毒感染方面已显示出潜在的辅助作用,改善抗体反应和体液免疫。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述的重点是通过检查益生菌的免疫调节作用和 抗病毒药物 机制,使用益生菌减轻家禽病毒感染,特别是 禽流感 A 病毒。核心问题是益生菌是否可以作为一种有效策略,通过人畜共患 传播 来减少这些病毒对家禽健康和潜在人类健康的影响。
证据基础与方法
该综述使用“益生菌”、“乳杆菌”、“嗜酸菌”、“家禽”、“病毒感染”、“新城疫病毒 (NDV)”等术语和其他相关术语在 Google Scholar、Scopus 和 PubMed 上进行了文献检索。纳入标准基于与家禽益生菌干预措施的相关性、对免疫机制的关注以及对西非家禽养殖系统中常见病毒病原体的检查。
共识与结论
益生菌,特别是嗜酸乳杆菌和植物乳杆菌,已显示出调节免疫参数、增强抗体反应和减少针对 禽流感 A 病毒 (AIV) 的病毒载量的潜力。这些发现表明,益生菌可能是改善家禽 AIV 免疫防御的可行策略。
争议与缺口
该综述强调需要对益生菌针对特定 禽流感 亚型(例如 H5N1 和 H5N6)的作用进行更有针对性的研究。关于益生菌在预防人类 AIV 人畜共患 传播 方面的长期功效的证据有限,需要进一步研究来解决这些差距。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先研究益生菌对特定 禽流感 亚型的功效及其在预防人畜共患 传播 中的作用。实际应用可能包括将益生菌融入家禽养殖实践中,以增强 免疫应答 并减少 AIV 暴发 的经济和公共卫生负担。

Probiotics as a Strategy for Mitigating Viral Infections in Poultry: Immunomodulatory Mechanisms and Therapeutic Potential.

Authors: Elmer Nayra Ametefe, Daniel Saka, Juliet Nuerkie Anang, Lourraine Sena Doh, Leonard Kofi Adjorlolo
Original abstract (English)
Poultry viral infections remain a persistent challenge for the poultry industry, causing significant economic losses and posing potential threats to human health. The escalating frequency of outbreaks involving pathogens, such as infectious bronchitis virus (IBV), Newcastle disease virus (NDV) and avian influenza virus (AIV), underscores the urgent need for effective interventions. Probiotics have gained attention as a promising strategy to combat these viral infections due to their potential in enhancing immune responses and establishing a healthy gut microbiome. A literature search was carried out on Google Scholar, Scopus and PubMed using key search terms, such as 'probiotics', 'Lactobacillus', 'acidophilus', 'poultry', 'viral infections', 'Newcastle Disease Virus (NDV)' and other relevant terms. Inclusion criteria for manuscripts used were based on the relevance to probiotic-based interventions in poultry, the focus on immunological mechanisms and the examination of viral pathogens commonly found in West African poultry farming systems. This review explores the role of probiotics in mitigating the effects of NDV, AIV and IBV in poultry, focusing on their immunomodulatory effects and antiviral mechanisms. Lactobacillus acidophilus, L. plantarum have shown an interesting promise in countering NDV infections by modulating immunological parameters and enhancing antibody responses. Similarly, these probiotics have exhibited antiviral activity against AIV, promoting immune activation and reducing viral loads. plantarum have emerged as potential adjuvants for IBV vaccines, improving antibody responses and humoural immunity.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the use of probiotics to mitigate viral infections in poultry, particularly avian influenza A viruses, by examining their immunomodulatory effects and antiviral mechanisms. The central question is whether probiotics can serve as an effective strategy for reducing the impact of these viruses on poultry health and potentially human health through zoonotic transmission.
Evidence base and method
The review conducted a literature search on Google Scholar, Scopus, and PubMed using terms like 'probiotics', 'Lactobacillus', 'acidophilus', 'poultry', 'viral infections', 'Newcastle Disease Virus (NDV)', and other relevant terms. Inclusion criteria were based on the relevance to probiotic-based interventions in poultry, the focus on immunological mechanisms, and the examination of viral pathogens common in West African poultry farming systems.
Consensus and conclusions
Probiotics, particularly Lactobacillus acidophilus and Lactobacillus plantarum, have shown potential in modulating immunological parameters, enhancing antibody responses, and reducing viral loads against avian influenza A viruses (AIV). These findings suggest that probiotics may be a viable strategy for improving immune defenses against AIV in poultry.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights the need for more targeted studies on probiotics' effects against specific avian influenza subtypes, such as H5N1 and H5N6. There is limited evidence on the long-term efficacy of probiotics in preventing zoonotic transmission of AIV to humans, and further research is required to address these gaps.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize investigating the efficacy of probiotics against specific avian influenza subtypes and their role in preventing zoonotic transmission. Practical applications may include integrating probiotics into poultry farming practices to enhance immune responses and reduce the economic and public health burden of AIV outbreaks.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

H5N1 禽流感 对已经减少的秃鹫种群构成全球威胁 - Bioengineer.org

H5N1 Avian Influenza Emerges as a Global Threat to Already Declining Vulture Populations - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报The article, published on October 28, 2026, highlights the growing threat of H5N1 avian influenza to global vulture populations. Vultures, already facing conservation concerns due to poisoning and other human-related threats, are now at risk from the highly contagious and high-fatality H5N1 virus. The virus has caused severe impacts, with thousands of American black vultures and Turkey vultures dying in the United States and South America. In Europe, the Bearded vulture has been significantly affected, and the critically endangered California condor saw a 6 percent population decline in six weeks. The virus exploits the scavenging behavior of vultures, increasing the risk of contagion as more carcasses become infected. Researchers warn that this could lead to a feedback loop, accelerating mortality in already vulnerable species. The letter, published in Conservation Biology, proposes five courses of action, including monitoring, vaccination campaigns, non-contaminated feeding stations, and tailored action plans. The authors stress the need for rapid, coordinated, and internationally concerted efforts to address this threat, as vultures play a crucial ecological role in preventing the spread of pathogens.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The article was published on October 28, 2026. The H5N1 panzootic is spreading widely in regions critical for vultures, with the situation being particularly alarming for the California condor, whose wild population fell by 6 percent in six weeks due to the outbreak.
地点与对象
H5N1 病毒已经影响了美国、南美洲、欧洲和美洲的秃鹫种群。受影响的物种包括美洲黑兀鹫、土耳其兀鹫、胡须兀鹫、加州秃鹰和安第斯秃鹰。
事件
H5N1 禽流感 病毒正在引发一场大流行,在对秃鹫至关重要的地区广泛传播并威胁其种群。正如《保护生物学》发表的一封信中所记录的那样,该事件得到了研究人员和保护主义者的证实。
规模、影响与风险
美国和南美洲已有数千只美洲黑秃鹫和土耳其秃鹫死亡。欧洲的胡秃鹫和美洲的加州秃鹰也受到了严重影响。该病毒在家养动物和野生动物中的死亡率都很高,而且随着更多秃鹫死亡,传染的风险也会增加,从而形成一个可能加速 死亡率 的反馈循环。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究人员提出了五项行动方案,包括监测秃鹫种群、实施 疫苗接种 运动、确保喂食站不受污染以及制定针对具体情况的行动计划。这封信由乔恩·莫兰特和其他研究人员牵头,目前正在做出回应,呼吁采取协调一致的国际行动。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 Avian Influenza Emerges as a Global Threat to Already Declining Vulture Populations - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
The article, published on October 28, 2026, highlights the growing threat of H5N1 avian influenza to global vulture populations. Vultures, already facing conservation concerns due to poisoning and other human-related threats, are now at risk from the highly contagious and high-fatality H5N1 virus. The virus has caused severe impacts, with thousands of American black vultures and Turkey vultures dying in the United States and South America. In Europe, the Bearded vulture has been significantly affected, and the critically endangered California condor saw a 6 percent population decline in six weeks. The virus exploits the scavenging behavior of vultures, increasing the risk of contagion as more carcasses become infected. Researchers warn that this could lead to a feedback loop, accelerating mortality in already vulnerable species. The letter, published in Conservation Biology, proposes five courses of action, including monitoring, vaccination campaigns, non-contaminated feeding stations, and tailored action plans. The authors stress the need for rapid, coordinated, and internationally concerted efforts to address this threat, as vultures play a crucial ecological role in preventing the spread of pathogens.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 30, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology H5N1 Avian Influenza Emerges as a Global Threat to Already Declining Vulture Populations by October 28, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 4 mins read 0 H5N1 Avian Influenza Emerges as a Global Threat to Already Declining Vulture Populations Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Vultures, one of the most threatened groups of birds in the world, now face a new and dangerous adversary: the highly pathogenic avian influenza virus H5N1. At least 16 of the 23 vulture species currently recognized are of conservation concern, largely as a result of poisoning and other threats stemming from human activity. Against this already precarious backdrop, the H5N1 virus, which has caused a panzootic, the animal equivalent of a pandemic, is spreading widely in regions that are critical for vultures, and researchers warn that it could push some populations toward collapse. A new letter published in the journal Conservation Biology sets out the scale of the problem and proposes concrete courses of action for conservationists and wildlife managers. The letter was led by Jon Morant, a researcher at the Department of Zoology and Animal Cell Biology of the EHU-University of the Basque Country, working together with several researchers at the Mediterranean Institute for Advanced Studies (IMEDEA) in Mallorca and the Institute for Research in Biodiversity and Environment (INIBIOMA) in Argentina. In addition to Morant, the signatories include Andrea Santangeli of IMEDEA, and Pablo Plaza and Sergio A. Lambertucci of INIBIOMA. Morant, who is also a lecturer and researcher at the Álava Campus of the EHU, is currently undertaking a research stay at the UCD School of Biology and Environmental Science in Dublin as part of a Basque Government postdoctoral programme. The stated aim of the letter is to alert the research community and those responsible for conservation so that they can establish coordinated actions against this emerging threat. The documented impacts of the current H5N1 panzootic on vultures are already severe. Thousands of American black vultures (Coragyps atratus) and Turkey vultures (Cathartes aura) have died in the United States and South America. The virus has also had a significant impact on the Bearded vulture (Gypaetus barbatus) in Europe, and on the California condor (Gymnogyps californianus) and the Andean condor (Vultur gryphus) in the Americas. The situation is particularly alarming for the California condor, a species that is critically endangered: its wild population fell by 6 percent in just six weeks as a result of the outbreak. Discover more Check Symptoms Get Cold Relief Book a Safari Study Biology birds The ecology of vultures makes them unusually vulnerable to this particular virus. In general, vultures are social animals that gather together at feeding stations, places where the risk of contagion is high because many individuals congregate in close proximity. As scavengers, vultures are resistant to many bacteria and pathogens, an adaptation that allows them to thrive on carrion that would be dangerous to other animals. However, Morant explains, this natural resilience is not enough to protect them from the H5N1 virus, which has a high fatality rate in both domestic and wild animals. Avian influenza is not exclusively a disease of birds. For vultures, the epidemiological logic is stark: the greater the number of infected carcasses in the environment, the greater the risk of contagion for the birds that feed on them. Because these carcasses can themselves become sources of infection, a paradox arises at the population level. As more vultures die, the capacity of the scavenger community to eliminate carrion is reduced, pathogens spread more widely through the environment, and the risk of contagion increases further. This feedback loop means that mortality can, in principle, accelerate rather than stabilize itself, a dynamic that is especially worrying for species already in decline. The threat carries consequences beyond the birds themselves. Vultures provide what Morant describes as one of the most important ecosystem services for human wellbeing: they feed on decomposing animal carcasses that harbour pathogens. It has been proven that scavengers help prevent or reduce the spread of pathogens, in other words, they prevent disease-causing microorganisms from continuing to spread through the environment. A decline in vulture populations therefore removes a natural barrier against the circulation of infectious agents, with potential implications for both wildlife health and human health. This functional role is a central reason why the authors argue that the threat of H5N1 to vultures deserves urgent and coordinated attention from the research and conservation communities. In response, the signatories of the letter call on conservationists and managers to adopt a holistic and multi-dimensional approach to reducing the threat posed to vultures by the H5N1 virus, and they set out five specific courses of action. The first is monitoring: the researchers consider it urgent to monitor vulture populations closely. Discover more Identify Plants Shop Bird Supplies Find Treatments Track Outbreaks biology The second proposed course of action is the implementation of vaccination campaigns when populations are at serious risk and where logistical conditions permit. Morant points to the United States as an example: in the case of the California condor, the research community has implemented a vaccination campaign in response to the high risk of the species becoming extinct. The third course of action concerns the feeding stations used for scavenger birds. Because these sites concentrate large numbers of vultures around supplied carcasses, the authors stress that it is important that the birds are fed with non-contaminated carcasses, so that supplementary feeding does not itself become a route of viral transmission. The fourth course of action is aimed at researchers themselves. The fifth recommendation is to develop and promote action plans adapted to each context. The countries where vultures live have different socio-economic situations and highly diverse governance models, and the authors argue that effective responses must therefore be tailored to local realities rather than imposed as a single uniform template. The signatories are openly concerned about the problem, and they hope that, while the letter remains open, researchers and conservation managers will begin to take action. Morant proposes using the letter as a guide, and concludes that to tackle this dangerous epidemiological situation it is urgent to develop coordinated cross-border plans and to integrate these actions into shared governance structures. The article, titled “Emerging threat of avian influenza to the world’s vultures,” was published in Conservation Biology on 19 August 2026. For a group of birds in which at least 16 of 23 species are already of conservation concern, the arrival of a highly contagious, high-fatality virus that exploits the very scavenging behaviour that makes vultures ecologically valuable represents a threat that the authors argue can only be met through rapid, coordinated and internationally concerted action. Subject of Research: The threat of H5N1 avian influenza to global vulture populations and proposed conservation responses Article Title: Avian influenza threatens vulture populations worldwide Article References: Avian influenza threatens vulture populations worldwide. The H5N1 virus has impacted vulture populations in the United States, South America, Europe, and the Americas. The affected species include American black vultures, Turkey vultures, Bearded vultures, California condors, and Andean condors.
Event
The H5N1 avian influenza virus is causing a panzootic, spreading widely in regions critical for vultures and threatening their populations. The event is confirmed by researchers and conservationists, as documented in a letter published in Conservation Biology.
Scale, impact and risk
Thousands of American black vultures and Turkey vultures have died in the United States and South America. The researchers have proposed five courses of action, including monitoring vulture populations, implementing vaccination campaigns, ensuring non-contaminated feeding stations, and developing context-specific action plans. 犹他州水貂养殖场禽流感:关于 H5N1 暴发、突变 风险和政府应对措施的了解

Utah mink farm bird flu: What to know about the H5N1 outbreak, mutation risks, and government response
新闻简报An outbreak of highly pathogenic H5N1 avian influenza has been spreading uncontrolled for over two months at a commercial mink fur farm in Payson, Utah. This marks the first documented detection of H5N1 bird flu in captive mink on American soil. Internal communications and veterinary records obtained through public records requests reveal the virus has killed thousands of animals, with a projected mortality rate of approximately 80 percent. Mink have lungs and airways remarkably similar to humans, featuring alpha-2,6 sialic acid receptors that influenza viruses use to infect human nasal passages and throats. Scientists warn that the virus could undergo genetic reassortment due to its susceptibility to both human seasonal flu and avian influenza, potentially creating a hybrid pandemic strain. However, state and federal regulators have not implemented depopulation, quarantine, or routine symptom surveillance for exposed farmhands, citing a lack of regulatory framework and compensation programs for mink. The response has been criticized as inadequate, with experts noting that other countries have swiftly culled mink during H5N1 outbreaks. The outbreak raises concerns about the risk of viral adaptation and transmission to humans, especially as the 2026–2027 human flu season has started earlier than usual.
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 已不受控制地传播了两个多月,2026-2027 年人类流感季节比平常提前到来(N1、N16)。
地点与对象
暴发 以犹他州佩森附近的 C&T Fenn 农场为中心,影响了数千只水貂,并可能导致农场工人接触 (N2)。
事件
高致病性 H5N1 暴发 病毒已在一家商业水貂养殖场不受控制地传播,内部通讯和兽医记录显示该病毒已导致数千只动物死亡(N1、N3)。
规模、影响与风险
The facility projects an 80 percent mortality rate among mink, and the virus has been confirmed to infect dairy cattle and lead to sporadic human infections in farmworkers (N3, N9). The risk of genetic reassortment and potential transmission to humans is highlighted due to mink's cellular architecture (N11, N13, N15).
应对、状态与不确定性
州和联邦监管机构没有减少牛群数量,没有实施具有法律约束力的检疫,也没有在暴露的农场工人中进行常规症状 监测。该应对措施被批评为不充分且缺乏遏制措施(N7、N23、N25、N27、N28、N36)。

Utah mink farm bird flu: What to know about the H5N1 outbreak, mutation risks, and government response

News Brief
An outbreak of highly pathogenic H5N1 avian influenza has been spreading uncontrolled for over two months at a commercial mink fur farm in Payson, Utah. This marks the first documented detection of H5N1 bird flu in captive mink on American soil. Internal communications and veterinary records obtained through public records requests reveal the virus has killed thousands of animals, with a projected mortality rate of approximately 80 percent. Mink have lungs and airways remarkably similar to humans, featuring alpha-2,6 sialic acid receptors that influenza viruses use to infect human nasal passages and throats. Scientists warn that the virus could undergo genetic reassortment due to its susceptibility to both human seasonal flu and avian influenza, potentially creating a hybrid pandemic strain. However, state and federal regulators have not implemented depopulation, quarantine, or routine symptom surveillance for exposed farmhands, citing a lack of regulatory framework and compensation programs for mink. The response has been criticized as inadequate, with experts noting that other countries have swiftly culled mink during H5N1 outbreaks. The outbreak raises concerns about the risk of viral adaptation and transmission to humans, especially as the 2026–2027 human flu season has started earlier than usual.
Original source text (English)
An outbreak of highly pathogenic H5N1 avian influenza has been spreading uncontrolled for more than two months across a commercial mink fur farm south of Salt Lake City, triggering severe criticism from infectious disease specialists who warn that governmental inaction is creating a dangerous breeding ground for potential human viral adaptation. The outbreak, centered at C&T Fenn Farms near Payson in Utah County, represents the first documented detection of H5N1 bird flu in captive mink on American soil. Internal communications and veterinary records obtained through public records requests by the Animal Welfare Institute (AWI) and investigated by ProPublica reveal that the virus has killed thousands of animals, with the facility projecting a total mortality rate of approximately 80 percent. Here’s something you should know about minks: They have lungs and airways remarkably similar on a cellular level to those of people. They’ve spread flus and infected humans before with COVID-19. Right now, bird flu is spreading on a Utah farm filled with thousands of them... 🧵 pic.twitter.com/e0BFksEnrC — ProPublica (@propublica) October 24, 2026 Yet, unlike federal protocols governing poultry and dairy operations, state and federal regulators have not depopulated the herd, enforced a legally binding quarantine, or conducted routine symptom surveillance among exposed farmhands. Flu researchers and biosecurity experts have described the administrative response as an alarming departure from global disease containment standards, warning that allowing the virus to circulate unhindered through dense mammal populations heightens the probability of genetic mutations capable of crossing into the human population. Why Scientists Consider Mink the 'Perfect Petri Dish' While the current H5N1 strain has infected dairy cattle across the country and led to sporadic human infections in farmworkers exposed to livestock, it has lacked the biological capability to transmit efficiently from person to person. Avian influenza viruses preferentially bind to alpha-2,3 sialic acid receptors predominantly found in the respiratory tracts of birds. Mink, however, represent a unique biological conduit: Human-Like Cellular Architecture: The upper respiratory tracts of mink feature an abundance of alpha-2,6 sialic acid receptors—the exact cellular gateway influenza viruses exploit to infect human nasal passages and throats. The upper respiratory tracts of mink feature an abundance of alpha-2,6 sialic acid receptors—the exact cellular gateway influenza viruses exploit to infect human nasal passages and throats. Aerosol Transmission Risks: Prior peer-reviewed research, including studies published in Nature Communications and Emerging Microbes & Infections, has demonstrated that mink can transmit respiratory viruses efficiently through aerosolized droplets when housed in close proximity. Prior peer-reviewed research, including studies published in Nature Communications and Emerging Microbes & Infections, has demonstrated that mink can transmit respiratory viruses efficiently through aerosolized droplets when housed in close proximity. The Reassortment Threat: Because mink are susceptible to both human seasonal flu and avian influenza, co-infection within a single host cell can trigger genetic reassortment—a process where two distinct viruses swap gene segments. With the 2026–2027 human flu season underway earlier than usual, scientists caution that coinfection could yield a novel, hybrid pandemic strain. Because mink are susceptible to both human seasonal flu and avian influenza, co-infection within a single host cell can trigger genetic reassortment—a process where two distinct viruses swap gene segments. With the 2026–2027 human flu season underway earlier than usual, scientists caution that coinfection could yield a novel, hybrid pandemic strain. The Dairy Connection: Genomic sequencing conducted by the U.S. Department of Agriculture (USDA) confirmed that the Utah mink were infected with the identical B3.13 genotype circulating in American dairy herds. Farm operators routinely feed raw livestock offal and unpasteurized slaughterhouse tissue to mink during summer months to accelerate pelt growth. "Mink to mink transmission is where the danger escalates," said Gail Hansen, a former state public health veterinarian and epidemiologist. "As long as it continues to go mink to mink, it becomes more and more likely that it could jump to a human." Regulatory Paralysis: No Cull, No Quarantine, and 'Verbal Holds' When avian influenza strikes commercial poultry, federal policy is absolute: detection of a single positive bird triggers the mandatory extermination ("depopulation") of the entire flock, with the federal government reimbursing producers through statutory indemnity funds. When European mink farms experienced H5N1 outbreaks in Spain (2022) and Finland (2023), agricultural ministries intervened rapidly, ordering the preemptive culling of hundreds of thousands of animals to eliminate the viral reservoir. In Utah, however, the response was marked by regulatory gaps and jurisdictional confusion: No Federal Authority: The USDA Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (APHIS) informed Utah state veterinarian Amanda Price that the agency maintains no regulatory framework, dedicated compensation program, or approved vaccines for farmed mink. The USDA Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (APHIS) informed Utah state veterinarian Amanda Price that the agency maintains no regulatory framework, dedicated compensation program, or approved vaccines for farmed mink. No Formal Quarantine: The Utah Department of Agriculture and Food (UDAF) declined to issue a formal administrative quarantine order. Officials opted instead for an unmonitored "verbal hold," barring the producer from moving animals, manure, or carcasses off-site, while conceding in internal memos they had "no idea what the criteria would be to release" a formal quarantine. The Utah Department of Agriculture and Food (UDAF) declined to issue a formal administrative quarantine order. Officials opted instead for an unmonitored "verbal hold," barring the producer from moving animals, manure, or carcasses off-site, while conceding in internal memos they had "no idea what the criteria would be to release" a formal quarantine. Physical Absence: State records confirm that Dr. Price never physically inspected the Payson facility during the two-month outbreak, relying entirely on telephone updates with the owner and satellite views from Google Maps. State records confirm that Dr. Price never physically inspected the Payson facility during the two-month outbreak, relying entirely on telephone updates with the owner and satellite views from Google Maps. Industry Influence: Records show agricultural liaisons warned that ordering a cull would bankrupt the producer and destroy decades of selective genetic breeding lines. "The fact that the first instinct wasn’t just to depopulate and figure stuff out later is very concerning to me," said Tom Peacock, an avian influenza virologist at the Pirbright Institute. "Every other country that has had H5N1 in fur farms, depopulation has happened pretty quickly." Secrecy and Worker Surveillance Failures State agencies also took active measures to restrict public awareness of the outbreak. Communications between UDAF and the Utah Department of Health and Human Services (DHHS) noted that the situation was placed under a "closer hold" than dairy or poultry cases to protect the producer's privacy and avoid alerting animal rights organizations. Even more troubling to public health epidemiologists is the lack of clinical oversight for farmworkers: The Monitoring Ban: Under a 2025 inter-agency operating agreement between Utah's agriculture and health departments, public health officials are prohibited from conducting active symptom monitoring of farm personnel unless human-to-human transmission is already established. Under a 2025 inter-agency operating agreement between Utah's agriculture and health departments, public health officials are prohibited from conducting active symptom monitoring of farm personnel unless human-to-human transmission is already established. Unexamined Symptoms : Agricultural workers on fur operations face high exposure from bites, scratches, and aerosolized spray while handling aggressive animals. During an on-site visit by investigative reporters, an employee at the facility was observed with visible conjunctivitis (eye redness)—a clinical symptom present in more than 90 percent of documented human H5N1 infections. : Agricultural workers on fur operations face high exposure from bites, scratches, and aerosolized spray while handling aggressive animals. During an on-site visit by investigative reporters, an employee at the facility was observed with visible conjunctivitis (eye redness)—a clinical symptom present in more than 90 percent of documented human H5N1 infections. No Diagnostic Testing: The farm owner attributed the irritation to environmental dust, and county health authorities declined to administer diagnostic PCR testing, stating that testing was unnecessary unless the worker developed secondary respiratory symptoms like fever or cough. Federal CDC guidance recommends that all individuals exposed to H5N1-infected mammals undergo active daily health monitoring for ten days following their last contact, with prompt testing for any ocular or respiratory abnormalities. A Shifting Political and Legislative Landscape The Utah outbreak has revived long-standing debates regarding the biosecurity vulnerabilities of the commercial fur trade. During the COVID-19 pandemic in 2020, SARS-CoV-2 ripped through nine Utah mink farms, killing nearly 10,000 animals and documenting confirmed transmission between captive mink, feral wildlife, and agricultural workers. In response to the current outbreak, animal protection organizations and public health coalitions are lobbying Congress to pass the Mink VIRUS Act (sponsored by Rep. Adriano Espaillat, D-N.Y.), which would establish a voluntary one-year federal buyout program to permanently phase out domestic mink farming. Conversely, provisions under consideration within the pending federal Farm Bill seek to repeal historic bans on subsidizing the fur sector, opening federal trade promotion funding to domestic pelt producers

华盛顿州禽流感 暴发 波及八个亚基马奶牛场,工人确诊为轻度 H5N1 病例 - 医学日报

Washington Bird Flu Outbreak Reaches Eight Yakima Dairy Herds as Worker's Mild H5N1 Case Is Confirmed - Medical Daily
新闻简报华盛顿州亚基马县的一名工人已确诊一例人类感染 H5N1 禽流感的轻症病例,此前 暴发 已影响了 8 个奶牛群。联邦和州实验室将该病毒鉴定为 H5N1,这是该州首例与乳制品工人有关的此类病例。该工人出现眼睛刺激和上呼吸道症状,但没有住院。公共卫生官员正在监测密切接触者,并向接触者和乳制品提供检测和防护设备。CDC 报告自 2024 年 2 月以来美国发生了 72 例人类 H5N1 病例,其中许多是乳制品和家禽工人。虽然公众面临的风险仍然很低,但建议乳制品工人及其家人采取预防措施。病毒来源和基因测序结果尚未披露,官方正在进一步调查。疾病预防控制中心已将此病例通知世界卫生组织,强调国际监测的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 于 2026 年 10 月 26 日得到确认,10 月 8 日报道了首次在奶牛中检测到 H5N1,10 月 15 日和 16 日又有更多牛群检测呈阳性。CDC 于 10 月 23 日发布了第 41 周的每周流感报告,并于 10 月 15 日宣布了人类病例。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在华盛顿州亚基马县,影响了 8 个奶牛群。受影响的人群包括风险较高的乳制品工人及其家人。
事件
华盛顿州亚基马县一家奶牛场的一名工人被确诊为人类感染 H5N1 禽流感的轻症病例。CDC 和州实验室将该病毒鉴定为 H5N1,暴发 与奶牛群有关。亚基马卫生区和疾病预防控制中心报告了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 在 9 天内影响了 8 个奶牛群。该工人症状轻微,没有住院。CDC 报告自 2024 年 2 月以来美国发生了 72 例人类 H5N1 病例,其中许多是乳制品和家禽工人。公众面临的风险被认为较低,但乳制品工人及其家人面临的风险较高。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生人员正在追踪受感染工人的密切接触者,以监测潜在的传播情况。正在向暴露的个人和乳品厂提供测试和防护设备。病毒来源和基因测序结果尚未公开。疾病预防控制中心已将此病例通知世界卫生组织。

Washington Bird Flu Outbreak Reaches Eight Yakima Dairy Herds as Worker's Mild H5N1 Case Is Confirmed - Medical Daily

News Brief
A mild case of human infection with H5N1 bird flu has been confirmed in a worker in Yakima County, Washington, following an outbreak that has affected eight dairy herds. Federal and state labs identified the virus as H5N1, marking the first such case linked to a dairy worker in the state. The worker experienced eye irritation and upper respiratory symptoms but was not hospitalized. Public health officials are monitoring close contacts and providing testing and protective equipment to exposed individuals and dairies. The CDC reported 72 human H5N1 cases in the U.S. since February 2024, many among dairy and poultry workers. While the risk to the general public remains low, dairy workers and their families are advised to take precautions. The source of the virus and genetic sequencing results have not been disclosed, and officials are investigating further. The CDC has notified the World Health Organization about the case, highlighting the need for international monitoring.

Healthy Living Washington Bird Flu Outbreak Reaches Eight Yakima Dairy Herds as Worker's Mild H5N1 Case Is Confirmed Published Oct 26, 2026 7:45 AM EDT By Dorothy Brooks Justin Sullivan | Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on WhatsApp Bird flu has been confirmed in eight dairy herds in Yakima County, Washington, and federal testing has confirmed that a worker exposed at one of those dairies was infected with H5N1. The worker did not have severe illness and was not hospitalized, and officials say the risk to the general public remains low. The CDC's weekly flu report for week 41, published October 23, said the adult dairy worker developed eye irritation and upper respiratory symptoms. State testing found an H5 virus, and CDC testing identified it as H5N1. It is the first H5 bird flu case linked to a dairy worker in Washington. For most households, the message is reassurance with a few simple precautions. The people facing real risk are dairy workers and their families, who may need protective gear, symptom checks, and fast access to testing as the outbreak spreads from farm to farm. Eight Herds in Nine Days Federal and state labs confirmed Washington's first H5N1 detections in dairy cattle on October 8, at two Yakima County farms. One more herd tested positive on October 15, followed by five more on October 16, according to the Yakima Health District's bird flu page. The first affected herds had consistently tested negative through routine milk surveillance and were tested after cows showed signs of illness, USDA said in its announcement. Officials have not said how the virus reached the farms, and genetic sequencing results that could show which strain is involved have not been made public. A Mild Case, Close Contacts and the Wider Picture The Yakima Health District announced the human case on October 15 and said the resident was isolating at home. Public health staff are following up with the person's close contacts to make sure the virus is not spreading between people. Health Officer Dr. Dave Atteberry said the district wants contacts to "know what symptoms to watch for and can access testing and treatment if needed." The district is also providing tests for exposed people who develop symptoms and helping dairies get protective equipment for workers. The CDC's bird flu situation summary listed 72 human H5 cases in the United States since February 2024 as of its October 15 update, many of them among dairy and poultry workers. The CDC says human-to-human spread has not been identified in the country, and the World Health Organization was notified of the Washington case under international health rules. The timing adds a wrinkle. Seasonal flu activity is higher than usual for this point in the season, and the CDC's latest report shows the highest week 41 flu hospitalization rate since the 2010-2011 season. Health officials urge exposed workers to get a seasonal flu shot, in part to lower the chance of being infected with seasonal and bird flu at the same time, which scientists worry could let the viruses swap genes. Symptoms, Raw Milk and Steps for Households Bird flu symptoms in people can include eye redness or irritation, fever, cough, sore throat, a runny or stuffy nose, muscle aches, headache, and fatigue, according to the CDC. Fever is not always present. Shortness of breath, confusion, or seizures are signs of more serious illness. Anyone with symptoms after contact with sick cattle, birds, or raw milk should call a health care provider and mention the exposure. Pasteurized milk and dairy products remain safe, local health officials say. Raw milk and unpasteurized dairy products can carry the virus and should be avoided, and pets should not be fed raw milk or raw pet food. Dairy workers should wear recommended eye protection, masks and gloves, and get a seasonal flu vaccine. Prescription flu antiviral drugs work best when started early, so exposed workers with symptoms should not wait to call. Households without farm contact do not need special precautions beyond avoiding sick or dead animals. October 23: The CDC's weekly flu report details the H5N1 confirmation in the dairy worker. October 16: Washington's herd count reaches eight. October 15: Yakima County health officials announce the human case. October 8: Washington confirms its first infected dairy herds. Herd counts could grow as testing continues. MedicalDaily will update the story as officials report new findings. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

科学家绘制 H9N2 禽流感血凝素上的两个新线性表位 - Bioengineer.org

Scientists Map Two New Linear Epitopes on H9N2 Bird Flu Hemagglutinin - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Scientists have identified and mapped two new linear B-cell epitopes on the hemagglutinin protein of H9N2 avian influenza virus, which are conserved and specific to this subtype. The study, published on 22 July 2026 in the journal Animal Diseases, was conducted by a team led by Dong Chen and Hongyun Wei, with Rui Luo as the corresponding author. They expressed the extracellular domain of HA from the H9N2 strain A/chicken/Hubei/222/2022 as a recombinant fusion protein and used monoclonal antibodies to locate the epitopes. The first epitope, FSSSRSYQ, was found in the vestigial esterase subdomain, while the second, NLYTRTDTT, resides in the receptor-binding subdomain. Neither antibody showed neutralizing activity, but the epitopes are valuable for diagnostic assays due to their conservation and surface exposure. H9N2 is a low-pathogenicity avian influenza virus that has become endemic in poultry across Asia, the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Europe, with notable presence in China since the 1990s. It causes economic losses by reducing egg production and impairing immune function, and occasionally spills over into mammals, including pigs, dogs, and humans. The research highlights the potential for improved serological detection of H9N2, which is crucial for field surveillance, vaccine strain selection, and outbreak containment.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年7月22日发表,研究结果于2026年10月29日报告。
地点与对象
The research focuses on H9N2 avian influenza virus, which is endemic in poultry across Asia, the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Europe, with particular mention of its emergence in China in the 1990s.
事件
科学家们在 H9N2 禽流感 病毒的血凝素蛋白上鉴定并绘制了两个新的线性 B 细胞表位,它们是保守的、表面暴露的且针对该亚型。
规模、影响与风险
H9N2 是一种低致病性 禽流感 病毒,通过减少产蛋量、损害免疫功能和增加继发感染的易感性,给家禽造成经济损失。它偶尔也会感染哺乳动物,包括猪、狗和人类,这凸显了其人畜共患的潜力。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究描述了通过分子和结构分析来识别表位,包括单克隆抗体生产、表位作图和诱变。这些发现基于实验室方法,除非直接应用于诊断或治疗开发,否则被认为是公共卫生应对措施的补充。

Scientists Map Two New Linear Epitopes on H9N2 Bird Flu Hemagglutinin - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
Scientists have identified and mapped two new linear B-cell epitopes on the hemagglutinin protein of H9N2 avian influenza virus, which are conserved and specific to this subtype. The study, published on 22 July 2026 in the journal Animal Diseases, was conducted by a team led by Dong Chen and Hongyun Wei, with Rui Luo as the corresponding author. They expressed the extracellular domain of HA from the H9N2 strain A/chicken/Hubei/222/2022 as a recombinant fusion protein and used monoclonal antibodies to locate the epitopes. The first epitope, FSSSRSYQ, was found in the vestigial esterase subdomain, while the second, NLYTRTDTT, resides in the receptor-binding subdomain. Neither antibody showed neutralizing activity, but the epitopes are valuable for diagnostic assays due to their conservation and surface exposure. H9N2 is a low-pathogenicity avian influenza virus that has become endemic in poultry across Asia, the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Europe, with notable presence in China since the 1990s. It causes economic losses by reducing egg production and impairing immune function, and occasionally spills over into mammals, including pigs, dogs, and humans. The research highlights the potential for improved serological detection of H9N2, which is crucial for field surveillance, vaccine strain selection, and outbreak containment.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 30, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Scientists Map Two New Linear Epitopes on H9N2 Bird Flu Hemagglutinin by October 29, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Scientists Map Two New Linear Epitopes on H9N2 Bird Flu Hemagglutinin Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Researchers have identified and precisely mapped two previously uncharacterized linear B-cell epitopes on the hemagglutinin protein of H9N2 avian influenza virus, offering new molecular targets for subtype-specific diagnostic assays. The study, published as an open-access Original Article in the journal Animal Diseases on 22 July 2026, describes the generation of two monoclonal antibodies against the viral HA protein, the fine mapping of the short continuous peptide segments they recognize, and a detailed analysis of the residues and structural contexts that govern antibody binding. Although neither antibody showed neutralizing activity, the authors argue that the epitopes themselves are valuable precisely because they are conserved, surface-exposed, and largely specific to the H9N2 subtype. H9N2 is a low-pathogenicity avian influenza virus that was first isolated from turkeys in Wisconsin, USA, in 1966. Since then it has become endemic in poultry across Asia, the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Europe, and in China it emerged in the 1990s and now ranks among the most prevalent subtypes in chickens and other domestic birds. While infections are typically associated with mild respiratory signs, the virus causes substantial economic losses by reducing egg production, impairing immune function, and increasing susceptibility to secondary bacterial and viral infections. H9N2 also infects a range of avian hosts and occasionally spills over into mammals, including pigs, dogs, and humans, underscoring its zoonotic potential. In addition, the virus frequently donates internal gene segments to other avian influenza subtypes such as H5N1, H7N9, and H10N8, facilitating the emergence of novel reassortant strains with pandemic potential. The hemagglutinin protein sits at the center of both immunity and diagnostics for influenza A viruses. HA functions as a homotrimer on the viral surface, with each monomer consisting of two disulfide-linked subunits: HA1, the globular head that binds sialic acid receptors, and HA2, the stem that mediates membrane fusion. Because HA is strongly immunogenic, it is the principal target of neutralizing antibodies and the backbone of most vaccine and diagnostic antigen design. However, frequent antigenic drift, particularly within the HA1 domain, can alter antibody recognition and erode both vaccine efficacy and the reliability of diagnostic tests. Linear epitopes, which consist of continuous amino acid sequences that remain recognizable even under denaturing conditions, are considered especially useful building blocks for peptide-based diagnostic assays. Discover more Biology textbook sales Study Biology hospital hospitals Learn Engineering In the new work, the team, led by Dong Chen and Hongyun Wei with corresponding author Rui Luo, expressed the extracellular domain of HA from the H9N2 strain A/chicken/Hubei/222/2022 as a recombinant fusion protein carrying a human IgG Fc tag. The HA-hFc protein was produced in HEK293 cells and purified by protein A affinity chromatography. SDS-polyacrylamide gel electrophoresis revealed a single band above the predicted molecular weight of roughly 90 kilodaltons, a shift consistent with glycosylation, and Western blotting with H9N2-specific mouse antiserum and an anti-human IgG reagent confirmed successful expression and purification of the immunogen. Immunization of BALB/c mice with this recombinant protein yielded two hybridoma clones, designated 9C12 and 9F4. Both monoclonal antibodies specifically recognized viral HA in H9N2-infected MDCK cells, as demonstrated by Western blotting and an indirect immunofluorescence assay, and both bound the recombinant antigen in ELISA. Functional testing, however, showed that neither antibody inhibited hemagglutination in a hemagglutination inhibition assay, and neutralization assays returned IC50 values greater than 50 micrograms per milliliter, indicating that no neutralizing activity was detectable in vitro. The antibodies therefore recognize antigenically accessible but non-neutralizing regions of the HA molecule, a profile the authors consider well suited to epitope mapping and diagnostic development rather than therapeutic or vaccine applications. To locate the exact binding sites, the researchers used a panel of N-terminal EGFP-tagged HA truncation constructs expressed in HEK293T cells and screened by Western blot. For 9C12, sequential subdivision narrowed the reactive region from the full HA1 domain, amino acids 19 to 338, down through fragments covering residues 96 to 195, 96 to 145, and 116 to 135, before overlapping short peptides defined the minimal linear epitope as the eight-residue sequence FSSSRSYQ at positions 123 to 130. The same strategy applied to 9F4 traced its binding site through HA1 subfragments of residues 176 to 279, 176 to 225, and 196 to 215, ultimately defining the minimal epitope as the nine-residue sequence NLYTRTDTT at positions 201 to 209. Alanine-scanning mutagenesis then resolved which individual residues within each epitope are essential for antibody recognition. Within the 9C12 epitope, replacing S124, S125, R127, S128, Y129, or Q130 with alanine abolished antibody binding, whereas substitutions at F123 and S126 had no detectable effect. For the 9F4 epitope, the indispensable residues were N201, Y203, T204, T206, and D207, while L202, R205, T208, and T209 could be replaced without disrupting recognition. These single-residue definitions, the authors note, provide a rational molecular basis for assay design, since the critical positions are predominantly hydrophilic or charged, consistent with their role as anchor points stabilizing the antibody-epitope interaction. Discover more Track Biotech Tour Colleges Genetic testing kits Health Laboratory supplies Structural and bioinformatic analyses placed both epitopes in functional context. Immunological profiling with the PROTEAN module of DNAStar showed that both peptides display strong hydrophilicity, high antigenic indices, and elevated surface probability. A three-dimensional homology model of the HA trimer, generated with SWISS-MODEL and based on the A/chicken/Hubei/222/2022 sequence, confirmed that both epitopes are exposed on the surface of the trimer and solvent accessible. Domain-level mapping revealed that the 9F4 epitope NLYTRTDTT lies within the receptor-binding subdomain, whereas the 9C12 epitope FSSSRSYQ resides in the vestigial esterase subdomain. Notably, even though the 9F4 epitope sits within the receptor-binding region, its recognition by 9F4 was insufficient to confer detectable neutralizing activity. Conservation analysis strengthened the diagnostic case for both motifs. Multiple sequence alignment of HA proteins from 30 representative H9N2 isolates retrieved from the NCBI database showed that the 9C12 epitope is largely conserved, with key residues F123, S124, S125, S128, Y129, and Q130 strongly stable and only minor substitutions observed at S126 and R127. The 9F4 epitope, by contrast, displayed near-complete invariance across all strains analyzed, which the authors describe as exceptional stability within the H9N2 subtype. When the same regions were compared with homologous sequences in other influenza A virus subtypes, including H1 through H13 and H16, both epitopes were poorly conserved in non-H9 viruses, suggesting subtype-associated conservation that could serve as a molecular marker for H9N2-associated detection and surveillance. The authors position these findings against the limitations of current serological tools. Traditional hemagglutination inhibition tests remain widely used but are often limited by cross-reactivity and insufficient resolution when distinguishing closely related subtypes such as H9, H5, and H7. Monoclonal antibodies directed against short, well-defined linear epitopes such as NLYTRTDTT may help improve the specificity of H9N2 serological detection, a capability relevant to field surveillance, vaccine strain selection, outbreak containment, and monitoring of antigenic evolution. The authors emphasize that because both epitopes are nonneutralizing, they are unsuitable for direct use in protective vaccine design, and they caution that future work must validate diagnostic performance across large-scale poultry samples and multiple assay platforms, including peptide-based ELISAs and lateral-flow immunoassays. Within those limits, the study delivers experimentally validated peptide motifs and paired monoclonal antibodies that expand the antigenic map of H9N2 hemagglutinin and provide concrete raw material for subtype-specific diagnostic reagents. 犹他水貂养殖场禽流感 暴发 暴露出反应差距

Utah Mink Farm Bird Flu Outbreak Exposes Response Gaps
新闻简报犹他州一家水貂养殖场爆发的禽流感 暴发 持续了两个多月,引发了人们对工人安全和政府应对措施缺陷的担忧。暴发 得到联邦 监测 数据的证实,有 7 只圈养水貂 H5N1 检测呈阳性。然而,感染或死亡动物的总数尚未独立确定。截至 2026 年 10 月 23 日,犹他州卫生当局报告没有确诊的人类感染病例,公众面临的风险仍然很低。该病毒与美国奶牛中发现的 H5N1 病毒株有关,专家对病毒在哺乳动物中的重排和适应表示担忧。官员们没有下令扑杀水貂,也没有积极监控接触水貂的工人,理由是缺乏专门的水貂监管计划,也没有获得批准的 疫苗。病毒进入农场的确切途径仍不清楚,有人呼吁在传播期间继续进行基因测序,以检测病毒的任何变化。CDC 建议在最后一次已知接触后 10 天内对可能接触过的个体进行症状监测,但由于对农场合作的担忧,犹他州官员此前修改了对工人的监测方法。
查看新闻五要素
时间
据报道,暴发 持续了两个多月,官员们于 2026 年 8 月中旬意识到水貂受感染,ProPublica 调查于 2026 年 10 月 23 日发布。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在美国犹他州的 C&T Fenn 农场,该农场饲养了数千只水貂以获取毛皮。受影响的人群包括水貂和可能接触病毒的农场工人。
事件
犹他州水貂养殖场报告了两个月的禽流感 暴发,其中 7 只圈养水貂 H5N1 检测呈阳性。该事件得到了联邦动物 监测 数据的证实,并强调了对工人安全和政府反应的担忧。
规模、影响与风险
暴发涉及数千只水貂,其中7例确诊为阳性。截至 2026 年 10 月 23 日,犹他州尚未报告确诊的人类感染病例,公众面临的风险仍然很低。该病毒与美国奶牛中传播的 H5N1 病毒有关,人们担心病毒在哺乳动物中的重排和适应。
应对、状态与不确定性
犹他州卫生当局没有下令扑杀受感染的水貂,也没有积极监测受感染的工人是否出现症状。联邦官员指出,缺乏针对水貂的专门监管计划以及针对水貂的经批准的禽流感 疫苗。病毒进入水貂养殖场的确切途径仍不确定,专家呼吁继续对新样本进行测序,以评估潜在的变化。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Utah Mink Farm Bird Flu Outbreak Exposes Response Gaps

News Brief
A bird flu outbreak at a Utah mink farm, which lasted over two months, has raised concerns about worker safety and gaps in government response. The outbreak was confirmed by federal surveillance data, with seven captive mink testing positive for H5N1. However, the total number of infected or dead animals was not independently established. Utah health authorities reported no confirmed human infections as of October 23, 2026, and the risk to the general public remains low. The virus was linked to H5N1 strains found in US dairy cattle, and experts expressed worries about viral reassortment and adaptation in mammals. Officials did not order culling of the mink or actively monitor exposed workers, citing the lack of a dedicated regulatory program for mink and no approved vaccine. The exact route of virus entry into the farm remains unclear, and there is a call for continued genetic sequencing to detect any changes in the virus during ongoing transmission. The CDC recommends monitoring potentially exposed individuals for symptoms for 10 days after their last known exposure, but Utah officials had previously modified their approach to worker monitoring due to concerns about farm cooperation.
Original source text (English)
