They also highlight the potential impact that the decline in vultures could have on ecosystems and human wellbeing. To address this situation, they propose five courses of action: to monitor vulture populations, implement vaccination campaigns, manage the carrion, establish protocols for handling vultures, and promote local action plans. Vultures are one of the most threatened group of birds in the world. Currently, at least 16 of the 23 species of vulture are of conservation concern, largely as a result of poisoning and other threats due to human activity. However, “they provide us with one of the most important ecosystem services for human wellbeing: they feed on decomposing animal carcasses, that harbour pathogens. It has been proven that scavengers help prevent or reduce the spread of pathogens. That is, they prevent disease-causing microorganisms from continuing to spread”, explains Jon Morant, researcher at the EHU-University of the Basque Country. The avian flu virus (H5N1), which caused the panzootic —the equivalent of a pandemic in animals—, is highly contagious and could lead to the collapse of vulture populations, which are already in decline. In fact, it has been confirmed that the virus has spread widely in certain areas of the world that are critical for vultures, which has had serious consequences. For example, thousands of American black vultures (Coragyps atratus) and Turkey vultures (Cathartes aura) have died in the United States and South America. The virus has also had a significant impact on the Bearded vulture (Gypaetus barbatus) in Europe and the California condor (Gymnogyps californianus) and Andean condor (Vultur gryphus) in America. It should be noted that the California condor is critically endangered and that its wild population fell by 6% in just six weeks. To warn of this threat and to establish courses of action to address the problem, Jon Morant, researcher at the Department of Zoology and Animal Cell Biology of the EHU, published a letter in the journal Conservation Biology, together with several researchers at the Mediterranean Institute for Advanced Studies (IMEDEA, Mallorca) and the Institute for Research in Biodiversity and Environment (INIBIOMA, Argentina). “We wanted to alert the research community and those responsible for conservation so that they could establish coordinated actions”, explains Morant. A paradox In general, “vultures are social animals and gather together at the feeding stations; that is, places where the risk of contagion is high. Moreover, they could contract the H5N1 virus if they consume infected animal carcasses. As scavengers, vultures are resistant to many bacteria and pathogens. However, this is not enough to protect them from the H5N1 virus”, explains Morant. The virus has a high fatality rate in both domestic and wild animals. Avian influenza does not exclusively affect birds: it has been documented that more than 48 species of mammal around the world have been infected. “The greater the number of infected carcasses, the greater the risk of contagion for the vultures. Since these carcasses can become sources of infection, a paradox arises: as more vultures die, the capacity to eliminate the carrion reduces, the pathogens spread and contagion increases”, explains the researcher. Five courses of action Researchers who sign the letter call on conservationists and managers to adopt a holistic and multi-dimensional approach to reducing the threat posed to vultures by the H5N1 virus. Firstly, they consider it urgent to monitor vulture populations. “In the Basque Country, the Griffon vulture population is in a healthy condition, but the Egyptian vulture is a more sensitive. We need to pay particular attention to migratory species”, explains Morant. Secondly, they propose implementing vaccination campaigns when populations are at serious risk and where logistical conditions permit. “In the United States, in the case of the California condor, the research community has implemented a vaccination campaign in response to the high risk of the species becoming extinct”, he added. The third course of action focuses on feeding stations for scavenger birds. “It is important that they are fed with non-contaminated carcasses.” The fourth course of action is aimed at researchers: “They must comply strictly with biosecurity protocols (use gloves, masks and protective equipment), especially when working with small populations or with threatened species.” Finally, they recommend developing and promoting action plans adapted to each context, since the countries where the vultures live have different socio-economic situations and highly diverse governance models. The signatories of the letter are concerned about the problem and hope that, while the letter remains open, researchers and conservation managers will start to take action. “We propose using the letter as guide. To tackle this dangerous epidemiological situation, it is urgent to develop coordinated cross-border plans and to integrate these actions into shared governance structures”, concludes Morant. Additional information In addition to Jon Morant from the EHU, Andrea Santangeli (IMEDEA), Pablo Plaza (INIBIOMA) and Sergio A. Lambertucci (INIBIOMA) also signed the card. Morant is a lecturer and researcher at the Álava Campus of the EHU, though he is currently undertaking a stay at the UCD School of Biology and Environmental Science, Dublin, as part of a Basque Government postdoctoral programme. Bibliographic reference Jon Morant, Andrea Santangeli, Pablo Plaza, Sergio A. A study by researchers from the EHU-University of the Basque Country, IMEDEA, and INIBIOMA highlights the threat of avian influenza (H5N1) to vulture populations, noting that the virus has spread widely in critical vulture habitats and caused serious consequences, including the death of thousands of vultures in the United States and South America. 格林县在发现疑似病例后发布禽流感警告 - 斯普林菲尔德新闻领袖

Greene County issues bird flu warning after suspected cases identified - Springfield News-Leader

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

爱荷华州和阿肯色州确认禽流感病例

Bird Flu Cases Confirmed in Iowa, Arkansas
新闻简报爱荷华州和阿肯色州均已确诊禽流感病例,特别是 H5N1 病例。在爱荷华州,萨克县的商业火鸡群中检测到了该病毒,促使州长 Kim Reynolds 于 2026 年 10 月 28 日发布灾难公告,动员州资源进行跟踪、遏制和清理。阿肯色州于 2026 年 10 月 27 日在巴克斯特县东部的一个后院家禽群中确认了 2026-27 季节的首例病例。这两个州的官员都敦促家禽饲养者加强生物安全,监测疾病或突然死亡的迹象,并立即报告疑似病例。虽然 暴发 正在进行中,但爱荷华州官员表示,检测结果不会造成直接的公共卫生风险,家禽产品被认为可以安全食用。该病毒具有高度传染性,当局强调将家禽饲养在室内并实施严格的生物安全协议以防止进一步传播的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年10月27日,阿肯色州巴克斯特县东部确诊禽流感病例,爱荷华州州长金·雷诺兹于2026年10月28日发布了灾难公告。
地点与对象
爱荷华州萨克县和阿肯色州巴克斯特县东部报告了这些病例,影响了商业火鸡群和后院家禽群。
事件
H5N1 禽流感在爱荷华州萨克县的商业火鸡群和阿肯色州巴克斯特县东部的后院家禽群中得到证实。爱荷华州州长发布灾难公告,分配州资源用于追踪、遏制和清理。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 是 2026-27 年季节的一部分,爱荷华州报告了 2026 年的第八例病例。官员表示,这些检测不会立即造成公共卫生问题,家禽产品仍然可以安全食用。
应对、状态与不确定性
两个州的官员都敦促鸡群主人加强生物安全,监测疾病或突然死亡情况,并及时报告疑似病例。该病毒具有高度传染性,当局强调需要采取生物安全措施并尽可能将家禽放在室内。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird Flu Cases Confirmed in Iowa, Arkansas

News Brief
Bird flu cases, specifically H5N1, have been confirmed in both Iowa and Arkansas. In Iowa, the virus was detected in a commercial turkey flock in Sac County, prompting Governor Kim Reynolds to issue a disaster proclamation on October 28, 2026, to mobilize state resources for tracking, containment, and cleanup. Arkansas confirmed its first case of the 2026–27 season on October 27, 2026, in a backyard poultry flock in eastern Baxter County. Officials in both states have urged poultry owners to enhance biosecurity, monitor for signs of illness or sudden deaths, and report suspected cases immediately. While the outbreak is ongoing, Iowa officials have stated that the detections pose no immediate public health risk, and poultry products are considered safe for consumption. The virus is highly contagious, and authorities have emphasized the importance of keeping poultry indoors and implementing strict biosecurity protocols to prevent further spread.
Original source text (English)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

阿肯色州农业部确认首例季节性 禽流感 病例 - KATV

Arkansas Department of Agriculture confirms first seasonal case of avian influenza - KATV
新闻简报The Arkansas Department of Agriculture confirmed the first seasonal case of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) in the state on October 28, 2026. The case was identified in a backyard domestic poultry flock in eastern Baxter County. HPAI is a highly contagious virus that can cause sudden death or severe illness in both wild and domestic birds. The disease spreads easily through contact with infected birds, contaminated equipment, or materials, and it poses a significant risk to the poultry industry, which is valued at $6.7 billion annually. Agriculture experts recommend strict biosecurity practices, including washing hands and shoes, limiting exposure to wild birds, restricting visitors, and quarantining new birds for at least two weeks. The department emphasized the importance of reporting suspected or confirmed cases and is actively monitoring the situation to prevent further spread.
查看新闻五要素
时间
阿肯色州农业部于2026年10月28日确认了第一例高致病性禽流感(HPAI)。事件日期为2026年10月28日,发布日期也是2026年10月28日。
地点与对象
该病例是在阿肯色州巴克斯特县东部的一个后院家禽群中确诊的。受影响的种群包括家禽和野生鸟类。
事件
阿肯色州农业部确认了该州首例季节性 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 病例。这是阿肯色州农业部报告的已确认事件。
规模、影响与风险
This is currently the only detection of HPAI in Arkansas. The disease can spread easily through contact with infected birds, contaminated equipment, or materials. It can also lead to major economic losses for the poultry industry, which is valued at $6.7 billion annually. The risk is explicitly stated as high due to the virus's contagious nature.
应对、状态与不确定性
阿肯色州农业部已确认该病例,并敦促家禽养殖户遵循严格的生物安全措施,以防止高致病性禽流感的传播。任何疑似或确诊病例都必须向有关部门报告。响应正在进行中,状态已确认。
